Yeah… I guess mostly at work. I don’t think my friends or family have really been affected much, at least not that I’ve noticed.

But with coworkers, or more like producers, I can tell there’s been a shift. They’re still nice to me, but I think they trust me less on the more complicated shoots now. And… I hate saying this because it sounds dramatic, but I’d been hoping to move toward a senior director of photography role, and that feels like it’s slipping away a bit. Not because I can’t do the creative or camera side, but because these mistakes make me look less reliable. Is that the kind of relationship problem you mean?

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I wouldn’t say my whole daily life is falling apart or anything. My relationships are okay, I think.

But at work, yeah, it’s starting to matter more than I want to admit. Some of the producers seem like they trust me less on the more complicated shoots now, and that’s… hard. I’ve been hoping to move toward a senior director of photography role, and it kind of feels like that chance is slipping, even though I know the actual camera work I do is good.

I don’t know if that counts as “significantly interfering,” but it feels like it’s affecting my path, if that makes sense.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart, but it’s been upsetting.

What finally scared me into coming in is that I keep making these dumb technical mistakes that I know better than to make — like labeling camera cards wrong, or missing a timecode note, or exporting a client cut with the wrong lower-third even after I thought I checked it.

It’s embarrassing, honestly, because these are not things I should be messing up at this point. So… yes, it is causing distress. Probably more than I’ve been admitting.

I’ve been hoping to move toward a senior director of photography role, and it kind of feels like that chance is slipping, even though I know the actual camera work I do is good.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as “significantly interfering,” but it feels like it’s affecting my path, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these mistakes at work are causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart, but it’s been upsetting.\n\nWhat finally scared me into coming in is that I keep making these dumb technical mistakes that I know better than to make — like labeling camera cards wrong, or missing a timecode note, or exporting a client cut with the wrong lower-third even after I thought I checked it.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because these are not things I should be messing up at this point. So… yes, it is causing distress. Probably more than I’ve been admitting.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the mistakes you've made at work when you're not there?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say repeatedly, exactly. I mean, I do think about them sometimes, especially if something reminded me of it, but I’m not sitting there all night replaying every mistake or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll feel embarrassed when it pops into my head, and then I try to move on. I hope that makes sense.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you find yourself having intrusive thoughts about your mistakes at work?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. I’m not sure I’d call them intrusive thoughts, exactly. Like, they don’t feel like they’re barging in constantly or anything.

It’s embarrassing, honestly, because I’m not careless about my work — at least I don’t want to be. But it’s happened enough that it scared me a little.\n\nDoctor: How have you been feeling about yourself since these mistakes started happening at work?\n\nPatient: I guess… not great, honestly. I’ve been trying not to make it into some huge thing, because everyone makes mistakes, right? But it does get to me.\n\nI feel embarrassed, mostly. Like I should be past this by now, or I should be able to just slow down and catch things. And then I get frustrated with myself because I know I’m capable of doing good work. I don’t want people thinking I’m sloppy.\n\nI’m not, like… falling apart or anything. But my confidence has definitely taken a hit. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since these mistakes started happening at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think sad or down most of the time, no. I mean, I get embarrassed and kind of beat myself up after something happens, but it’s more like frustration or anxiety about looking incompetent, if that makes sense. I’m still functioning and having okay moments. I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating on your work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t do my job, because I can, but I’m having trouble staying locked in on the details.\n\nThat’s kind of what scared me into coming in — I keep making these dumb technical mistakes I really do know better than to make. Like labeling camera cards wrong, missing a timecode note, or exporting a client cut with the wrong lower-third even after I thought I’d checked it. It feels like my focus slips at the exact wrong moment, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been forgetting things that you would normally remember?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… not in a big, obvious way, I don’t think. Like, I’m not forgetting major things or anything that dramatic.\n\nMaybe little stuff here and there, but I don’t know if that’s more normal life stress or me just not paying close enough attention in the moment. I wouldn’t want to say yes if it’s not really that. What kind of forgetting do you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing a loss of interest in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I still enjoy the things I usually like when I actually get to them. I mean, maybe I’ve been a little preoccupied or tired after work sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest exactly. Does that distinction make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? I don’t think so… I mean, I get stressed, sure, especially when I feel like I’m not keeping up or I’ve missed something, but I wouldn’t say I feel anxious all the time.\n\nAt least, I don’t think that’s the main thing. Is that the kind of anxiety you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain work tasks or situations because of your concerns about making mistakes?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t want to make it sound like I’m refusing to do my job or anything. It’s more that I’ve started kind of dreading certain technical parts because I keep making these dumb mistakes I really should know better than to make.\n\nLike labeling camera cards wrong, or missing a timecode note, or exporting a client cut and then realizing the lower-third was the wrong one, even after I thought I checked it. That’s honestly what scared me into coming in, because it feels embarrassing and I know those details matter. Does that count as avoiding, or is that more just… being worried about messing up?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends, family, or coworkers since these mistakes started happening at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess mostly at work. I don’t think my friends or family have really been affected much, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nBut with coworkers, or more like producers, I can tell there’s been a shift. I don’t know if that’s guilt exactly, or just… being responsible?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your mind has been foggy or unclear lately?\n\nPatient: Um… foggy? I’m not sure. I mean, I get embarrassed and kind of beat myself up after something happens, but it’s more like frustration or anxiety about looking incompetent, if that makes sense. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t do my job, because I can, but I’m having trouble staying locked in on the details.\n\nThat’s kind of what scared me into coming in — I keep making these dumb technical mistakes I really do know better than to make. Like labeling camera cards wrong, missing a timecode note, or exporting a client cut with the wrong lower-third even after I thought I’d checked it. It feels like my focus slips at the exact wrong moment, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been forgetting things that you would normally remember?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… not in a big, obvious way, I don’t think. Like, I’m not forgetting major things or anything that dramatic.\n\nMaybe little stuff here and there, but I don’t know if that’s more normal life stress or me just not paying close enough attention in the moment. I wouldn’t want to say yes if it’s not really that. What kind of forgetting do you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing a loss of interest in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I still enjoy the things I usually like when I actually get to them. I mean, maybe I’ve been a little preoccupied or tired after work sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest exactly. Does that distinction make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? I don’t think so… I mean, I get stressed, sure, especially when I feel like I’m not keeping up or I’ve missed something, but I wouldn’t say I feel anxious all the time.\n\nAt least, I don’t think that’s the main thing. Is that the kind of anxiety you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain work tasks or situations because of your concerns about making mistakes?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t want to make it sound like I’m refusing to do my job or anything. It’s more that I’ve started kind of dreading certain technical parts because I keep making these dumb mistakes I really should know better than to make.\n\nLike labeling camera cards wrong, or missing a timecode note, or exporting a client cut and then realizing the lower-third was the wrong one, even after I thought I checked it. That’s honestly what scared me into coming in, because it feels embarrassing and I know those details matter. Does that count as avoiding, or is that more just… being worried about messing up?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends, family, or coworkers since these mistakes started happening at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess mostly at work. I don’t think my friends or family have really been affected much, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nBut with coworkers, or more like producers, I can tell there’s been a shift. They’re still nice to me, but I think they trust me less on the more complicated shoots now. Like, I’ll still get positive feedback on my actual camera work, but when it comes to bigger, more detail-heavy setups, I feel like I’m not the first person they think of anymore.\n\nAnd… I hate saying this because it sounds dramatic, but I’d been hoping to move toward a senior director of photography role, and that feels like it’s slipping away a bit. Not because I can’t do the creative or camera side, but because these mistakes make me look less reliable. Is that the kind of relationship problem you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen at work?\n\nPatient: I mean… guilty sometimes, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d say often. Mostly I feel embarrassed or like I’ve let people down when something gets missed.\n\nI try not to dwell on it too much, because everyone makes mistakes, right? But when it affects someone else’s timeline or makes extra work for a producer, I do feel bad about that. I don’t know if that’s guilt exactly, or just… being responsible?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your mind has been foggy or unclear lately?\n\nPatient: Um… foggy? I’m not sure. I don’t think I’d describe it that way, exactly.\n\nI mean, I have moments where I’m not as sharp as I’d like to be, but “foggy or unclear” sounds more like… I don’t know, like a haze? What do you mean by foggy?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not the same person you were before these mistakes started happening at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big, dramatic way. I still feel like me.\n\nI guess I’m a little more cautious or self-conscious at work now, because I don’t want to make things worse or seem careless. But I wouldn’t say I feel like a totally different person. Maybe just… a bit shaken confidence-wise? If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep since these mistakes started happening at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed a big change, no. I mean, maybe if I’m worrying about work I’ll be a little more aware of it, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nMy sleep seems… mostly the same? I haven’t been tracking it or anything, so I might be missing something, but nothing has jumped out to me as different. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like these mistakes at work are significantly interfering with your ability to function in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say my whole daily life is falling apart or anything. I’ve been hoping to move toward a senior director of photography role, and it kind of feels like that chance is slipping, even though I know the actual camera work I do is good.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as “significantly interfering,” but it feels like it’s affecting my path, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these mistakes at work are causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart, but it’s been upsetting.\n\nWhat finally scared me into coming in is that I keep making these dumb technical mistakes that I know better than to make — like labeling camera cards wrong, or missing a timecode note, or exporting a client cut with the wrong lower-third even after I thought I checked it.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because these are not things I should be messing up at this point. So… yes, it is causing distress. Probably more than I’ve been admitting.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the mistakes you've made at work when you're not there?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say repeatedly, exactly. I mean, I do think about them sometimes, especially if something reminded me of it, but I’m not sitting there all night replaying every mistake or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll feel embarrassed when it pops into my head, and then I try to move on. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having intrusive thoughts about your mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call them intrusive thoughts, exactly. Like, they don’t feel like they’re barging in constantly or anything.\n\nIt’s more that if I’m reminded of a mistake, I’ll get that embarrassed feeling and think, “Ugh, why did I do that?” But then I can usually redirect. Does that answer what you mean by intrusive?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not for two weeks straight or most of the day. I mean, I’ve had days where I felt really down or embarrassed about work stuff, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nSo, no — I don’t think I’ve had a full period like that. I still enjoy the things I usually like when I actually get to them. I mean, maybe I’ve been a little preoccupied or tired after work sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest exactly. Does that distinction make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? I don’t think so… I mean, I get stressed, sure, especially when I feel like I’m not keeping up or I’ve missed something, but I wouldn’t say I feel anxious all the time.\n\nAt least, I don’t think that’s the main thing. Is that the kind of anxiety you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain work tasks or situations because of your concerns about making mistakes?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t want to make it sound like I’m refusing to do my job or anything. It’s more that I’ve started kind of dreading certain technical parts because I keep making these dumb mistakes I really should know better than to make.\n\nLike labeling camera cards wrong, or missing a timecode note, or exporting a client cut and then realizing the lower-third was the wrong one, even after I thought I checked it. That’s honestly what scared me into coming in, because it feels embarrassing and I know those details matter. Does that count as avoiding, or is that more just… being worried about messing up?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends, family, or coworkers since these mistakes started happening at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess mostly at work. I don’t think my friends or family have really been affected much, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nBut with coworkers, or more like producers, I can tell there’s been a shift. They’re still nice to me, but I think they trust me less on the more complicated shoots now. Like, I’ll still get positive feedback on my actual camera work, but when it comes to bigger, more detail-heavy setups, I feel like I’m not the first person they think of anymore.\n\nAnd… I hate saying this because it sounds dramatic, but I’d been hoping to move toward a senior director of photography role, and that feels like it’s slipping away a bit. Not because I can’t do the creative or camera side, but because these mistakes make me look less reliable. Is that the kind of relationship problem you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen at work?\n\nPatient: I mean… guilty sometimes, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d say often. Mostly I feel embarrassed or like I’ve let people down when something gets missed.\n\nI try not to dwell on it too much, because everyone makes mistakes, right? But when it affects someone else’s timeline or makes extra work for a producer, I do feel bad about that. I don’t know if that’s guilt exactly, or just… being responsible?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your mind has been foggy or unclear lately?\n\nPatient: Um… foggy? I’m not sure. I don’t think I’d describe it that way, exactly.\n\nI mean, I have moments where I’m not as sharp as I’d like to be, but “foggy or unclear” sounds more like… I don’t know, like a haze? And I don’t think I’ve noticed that. Maybe I’m splitting hairs, sorry. What do you mean by foggy?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not the same person you were before these mistakes started happening at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big, dramatic way. I still feel like me.\n\nI guess I’m a little more cautious or self-conscious at work now, because I don’t want to make things worse or seem careless. But I wouldn’t say I feel like a totally different person. Maybe just… a bit shaken confidence-wise? If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep since these mistakes started happening at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed a big change, no. I mean, maybe if I’m worrying about work I’ll be a little more aware of it, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nMy sleep seems… mostly the same? I haven’t been tracking it or anything, so I might be missing something, but nothing has jumped out to me as different. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like these mistakes at work are significantly interfering with your ability to function in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say my whole daily life is falling apart or anything. My relationships are okay, I think.\n\nBut at work, yeah, it’s starting to matter more than I want to admit. Some of the producers seem like they trust me less on the more complicated shoots now, and that’s… hard. I’ve been hoping to move toward a senior director of photography role, and it kind of feels like that chance is slipping, even though I know the actual camera work I do is good.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as “significantly interfering,” but it feels like it’s affecting my path, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these mistakes at work are causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart, but it’s been upsetting.\n\nWhat finally scared me into coming in is that I keep making these dumb technical mistakes that I know better than to make — like labeling camera cards wrong, or missing a timecode note, or exporting a client cut with the wrong lower-third even after I thought I checked it.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because these are not things I should be messing up at this point. So… yes, it is causing distress. Probably more than I’ve been admitting.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the mistakes you've made at work when you're not there?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say repeatedly, exactly. I mean, I do think about them sometimes, especially if something reminded me of it, but I’m not sitting there all night replaying every mistake or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll feel embarrassed when it pops into my head, and then I try to move on. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having intrusive thoughts about your mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call them intrusive thoughts, exactly. Like, they don’t feel like they’re barging in constantly or anything.\n\nIt’s more that if I’m reminded of a mistake, I’ll get that embarrassed feeling and think, “Ugh, why did I do that?” But then I can usually redirect. Does that answer what you mean by intrusive?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not for two weeks straight or most of the day. I mean, I’ve had days where I felt really down or embarrassed about work stuff, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nSo, no — I don’t think I’ve had a full period like that. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing significant distress or impairment in social or occupational functioning as a result of your work mistakes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, because I’m still doing my job and I think my camera work is good.\n\nBut I have noticed it affecting things. Some producers seem a little less willing to trust me with the more complicated shoots now, and that’s… hard to admit. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
